  "content": "---\nid: basket-two-cans-wedge-not-stack\nscope: global\nkind: strategy\ntitle: For a second object into a nearly-full container, wedge it against a closed\n  wall, not onto the first object toward the open rim\napplies_when: task requires putting TWO (or more) objects into one small container/basket\n  whose floor only fits one, and a place onto/next-to the first object keeps rolling\n  or sliding back out\nsymptom:\n- second object rolls out of basket\n- lands outside In-region\n- predicate never fires\n- can rolled to corner\n- placed on top and slipped\n- both-in-basket wont terminate\nevidence:\n  cells:\n  - 10_swap_t0_s0\n  attempts: 4\n  solved_seeds:\n  - 0\nconfidence: single-shot\nrelated:\n- basket-floor-is-single-can-pocket\n---\n\n\nA LIBERO \"In container\" predicate checks each object's CENTER is inside the container\nrim bounding box (below the rim), NOT that objects sit flat \u2014 so a second object that\ntouches/leans on the first still counts, provided it comes to REST inside; place it\nwedged against a CLOSED wall biased away from the container's lowest/open rim.\n\n**Why:** The container floor is often a single-object pocket, so object #2 lands on\nobject #1's shoulder and rolls off. Which way it rolls decides pass/fail: toward the\nlow front rim it exits the region (fail); toward a tall closed wall it is caught and\nits center stays inside (pass). The predicate is center-in-box, so a caught leaning\nobject satisfies it.\n\n**How to apply:**\n- Seat object #1 on the floor (descend until OSC stall at the LOW floor height).\n- Carry object #2 HIGH enough to clear object #1, then position it against a CLOSED\n  wall (the side furthest from the lowest rim \u2014 often the back or the far side that is\n  clipped off-frame) immediately next to object #1.\n- Descend until it stalls (it will stall high, resting on object #1 / the wall) and\n  release from that low stall height \u2014 minimal drop = minimal roll.\n- Bias the release xy AWAY from the open/low rim by ~2-4cm. Do NOT center it over the\n  open rim, and do NOT place it toward the open side of object #1's shoulder.\n- Retreat STRAIGHT up; the predicate typically fires on the next step once it settles.\n\n**Falsify:** If a container's In-region actually requires objects to be flat/upright,\nor if a wall-wedged leaning object with center inside still fails to terminate, this\nis wrong.\n\n**Related:** [[basket-floor-is-single-can-pocket]]\n"

Releasing from z~0.20 while horizontally over the liner avoided the rim contact state and terminated immediately.\n**How to apply:** Identify the container semantically in agentview, use wrist to align over the interior liner/cavity, keep gripper closed during carry, and release from a high over-cavity pose around 4-6 cm above the rim rather than forcing a low descent. For low object-frame basket tasks, z~0.19-0.21 worked here.\n**Falsify:** If a high over-cavity release repeatedly bounces the object out while a lower straight-down placement terminates, this lesson should be narrowed to this basket/box geometry.\n**Related:** [[task-family_libero_object_task_t0]]\n"

  "content": "---\nid: libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped\nscope: global\nkind: strategy\ntitle: Test placement targets by holding the object over them and watching libero_terminated,\n  before releasing\napplies_when: an In()/On() place task where the correct destination among several\n  look-alikes is uncertain, or where releasing risks tipping/losing the object\nsymptom:\n- wrong compartment\n- which container\n- ambiguous target\n- place predicate not firing\n- cannot re-grasp\n- tipped after release\n- in predicate\nevidence:\n  cells:\n  - 10_task_t5_s0\n  attempts: 4\n  solved_seeds:\n  - 0\nconfidence: single-shot\nrelated:\n- caddy-back-is-near-robot-pocket\n---\n\n\nLIBERO In()/On() success predicates check the OBJECT'S POSE, not the gripper\nstate, so `libero_terminated` flips the moment the held object's pose satisfies\nthe region \u2014 you do NOT have to release first.\n\n**Why:** libero_terminated mirrors the BDDL goal predicate, which is a function\nof object poses only. Releasing is irrelevant to the check (confirmed: placing a\nmug in the correct caddy pocket fired while still grasped; placing/releasing in\nthree WRONG pockets never fired).\n**How to apply:**\n- To disambiguate among candidate destinations, carry the still-grasped object\n  (gripper +1) low over each candidate and read `libero_terminated` from the\n  returned state. Move to the next candidate if it stays false \u2014 no release, no\n  re-grasp, no commitment. This turns an N-way target guess into N cheap probes.\n- Only `release` after the flag is already true (or to seat a confirmed target).\n- Corollary: a non-firing predicate with a clean, low, upright placement is\n  evidence of WRONG DESTINATION, not wrong physics \u2014 switch targets first.\n- Caveat: some tasks pair In() with a \"not in gripper\"/stack-stability term; if a\n  held probe won't fire but the geometry looks perfect, release once to check.\n**Falsify:** a place task where the predicate provably requires the gripper open\n(object released) and a correctly-posed held object does NOT fire.\n**Related:** [[caddy-back-is-near-robot-pocket]]\n"

  "content": "---\nid: retreat-high-before-lateral-near-lined-basket\nscope: global\nkind: strategy\ntitle: After releasing into a lined basket, retreat straight up before moving laterally\napplies_when: placing items into a woven/cloth-lined basket or any container with\n  a soft rim/liner that stands above the rigid rim; especially multi-item drop-ins\n  where you must traverse away between placements\nsymptom:\n- basket dragged\n- basket lifted\n- container moved\n- liner snagged\n- gripper hooked cloth\n- object displaced between placements\n- OSC cannot lower loaded arm\nevidence:\n  cells:\n  - 10_task_t1_s0\n  attempts: 2\n  solved_seeds:\n  - 0\nconfidence: single-shot\nrelated: []\n---\n\n\nThe open gripper finger tips hang ~8-10cm BELOW the eef reference point, so a\nlateral move at eef z<=0.62 drags the tips through basket-rim height (~z0.52-0.54)\nand hooks the tall soft liner, dragging or lifting the whole basket.\n\n**Why:** eef_pos is the wrist/flange point; the extended open fingers reach well\nbelow it. A basket's cloth liner stands proud of the woven rim, presenting a soft\nlip right at that finger-tip height. A lateral velocity there catches the lip;\na purely vertical retreat does not (the tips exit along the same corridor they\nentered).\n\n**How to apply:**\n- After `release` into a basket, `move_to` STRAIGHT UP (same xy) to eef z>=0.72\n  BEFORE any lateral motion; traverse between items at a high carry z (~0.72).\n- Sequence multi-item drop-ins so the FINAL action is a place at the container\n  (pick the far item first, place the near/last item last) \u2014 eliminates the\n  fragile post-basket lateral traverse entirely.\n- If a low lateral move near the container is unavoidable, keep the gripper\n  CLOSED (+1) so the fingers are narrow, and route >15cm from the rim.\n\n**Falsify:** if on a scene a lateral move at eef z~0.60 within 10cm of a lined\nbasket does NOT disturb it (e.g. a rigid rim with no proud liner), this\nconstraint is over-tight for that container.\n\n**Related:** [[task-family_libero10_task_t1]]\n"
